## 2026-05-20 Remaining 65 tok/s Route Audit

Current verified steady decode is still about `59-60 tok/s`, so 65 tok/s needs roughly `1.4-1.7 ms/token` depending on which baseline sample is used.

Routes with measured full-decode or repeated PROFILE evidence:

```text
selected gate/up local knobs       rejected or kept, remaining local deltas are us/layer scale
selected down local knobs          deferred exact reduce kept; WPR1/r32w1 rejected in full Kimi
router topk                        warp32 kept; bitonic/thread/writeback/linear-score rejected
router linear fusion               rejected; custom router GEMV is much slower than generated linear
shared gate/up                     grouped/xpack/loadvec rejected or too small
shared down/add                    generated path already fuses useful tail; custom path rejected
output argmax                      custom single-device kept; xpack/TP rejected for speed
Q4 preshuffle                      real but only ~0.2 ms/token best case and loader-heavy
MLA attn_output Q8                 full Kimi rejected despite standalone signal
MLA q/v/softmax/cache/layout       rejected in full Kimi or repeated microbench
dual MLA RMS                       standalone signal, full Kimi tied/slower, rejected
selected-MoE EP/TP                 best theoretical exact signal, but realistic distinct-weight path no speed proof
```

Implication:

- There is no remaining obvious `amd_kimi.py` single-kernel knob with enough headroom to bridge the gap.
- The only measured exact route near `~1 ms/token` was selected-MoE EP, but the realistic compact 6GPU path lost the speed proof and the full duplicated path hit AMD graph/runtime faults.
- The next serious attempt should either be a new selected-MoE EP design that proves speed on distinct real layers without full helper duplication, or a larger format/layout project that changes expert weight representation at load time. More local custom kernels should require a fresh profile-visible bucket and a plausible `>0.2 ms/token` upside before implementation.

Full no-profile sanity check:

```text

16.70 ms, 59.88 tok/s
16.67 ms, 59.98 tok/s
16.95 ms, 58.99 tok/s
16.80 ms, 59.53 tok/s
```

## 2026-05-20 Q4 Payload Load16 Rejected

Question: Q4_0 blocks are 18 bytes, so the payload starts at `base + 2`. The production kernels use `uint32_t` loads from `gb/wb + 2 + j`, which are half 4-byte aligned and half 2-byte misaligned. Could two aligned `uint16_t` loads, or explicit `u8` packing, beat the current 32-bit load?

Added durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_q4_payload_load16.py
```

The bench patches only the Q4 payload load in the current production selected gate/up and selected down sources:

```text
current: *reinterpret_cast<const uint32_t*>(p)
u16:     uint16_t(p[0:2]) | uint16_t(p[2:4]) << 16
u8:      p[0] | p[1] << 8 | p[2] << 16 | p[3] << 24
```

Down command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. SECTION=down EXPERTS=384 ITERS=128 \
```


PROFILE rejected the apparent wall signal:

```text

kimi_down_payload_u16     n=131 med=27.000 us avg=27.627 us

```

Gate command:


Decision: reject. The 32-bit payload load is not a meaningful bottleneck. `u16` is slightly slower, and `u8` is at best a sub-`0.2 us/call` average wobble with no median win. No production change.

## 2026-05-20 Down Direct XQ Rejected

Question: current `kimi_down_reduce_q4_q8_0` stages the whole `gate_up_q8` intermediate into LDS as prepacked `xps` plus `xss` for every down CTA. Since the buffer is only about `17KB`, can we skip LDS staging and let all CTAs read it directly from global/L2?

Added durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_down_direct_xq.py
```

The direct variant keeps the same `ROWS=8, WARPS_PER_ROW=2`, same deferred exact top-k reduction, and same Q4 add/xor unpack, but removes:

```text
__shared__ int xps[XQ_BLOCKS * 8]
__shared__ float xss[XQ_BLOCKS]
initial xps/xss staging loop
initial __syncthreads()
```

It instead repacks Q8 activation bytes directly inside the down GEMV loop.

Command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. EXPERTS=384 ITERS=256 \
python3 /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_down_direct_xq.py
```

## 2026-05-20 Shared Gate/Up Row Group Kept

Question: `kimi_shared_gate_up_q8_0` used `THREADS=32`, one output row per CTA. On gfx942 the hardware wavefront is still 64 lanes, so a 32-thread CTA uses half a wave. Earlier shared grouped tests used a heavy 32-row CTA with xq staging/prepacking and were slower. This test keeps the current math and direct Q8 reads, but groups a small number of output rows per CTA so each wave carries multiple independent 32-lane row reductions.

Added durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_shared_gateup_rowgroup.py
```

Initial command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. ITERS=512 \
python3 /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_shared_gateup_rowgroup.py
```

Initial PROFILE before production patch:

```text
kimi_shared_gate_up_q8_0 n=515 med=19.120 us avg=19.361 us
kimi_shared_gateup_r2    n=515 med=18.600 us avg=18.847 us
kimi_shared_gateup_r4    n=515 med=17.680 us avg=17.955 us
kimi_shared_gateup_r8    n=515 med=17.280 us avg=17.525 us
kimi_shared_gateup_r16   n=515 med=22.720 us avg=22.954 us
kimi_shared_gateup_r32   n=515 med=34.240 us avg=34.510 us
```

Integrated `ROWS=8` into production `kimi_shared_gate_up_q8_0`:

```text
THREADS = ROWS * 32
row = blockIdx.x * ROWS + (threadIdx.x >> 5)
lane = threadIdx.x & 31
for block = lane; block < DIM/32; block += 32
```

Post-patch microbench:

```text
kimi_shared_gate_up_q8_0 n=515 med=17.280 us avg=17.508 us
kimi_shared_gateup_r8    n=515 med=17.520 us avg=17.809 us
```

Full Kimi sanity:

```sh

CUSTOM_KIMI_MOE_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_SHARED_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ROUTER_TOPK=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_OUTPUT_ARGMAX_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ATTN_QAKV_HALF=1 \
PYTHONPATH=. python3 tinygrad/llm/cli.py \
  --model /raid/gguf/kimi/Kimi-K2.6-UD-Q4_K_XL-00001-of-00014.gguf \
  --shard 3 --benchmark 8
```

Steady samples:

```text

16.42 ms, 60.90 tok/s
16.34 ms, 61.18 tok/s
16.48 ms, 60.68 tok/s
16.54 ms, 60.46 tok/s
```

Decision: keep. This is a small exact/local win, about `1.8 us/layer` in the shared gate/up kernel and roughly `0.1 ms/token`. It does not reach 65 tok/s alone, but the full benchmark now shows stable `~60-61 tok/s` instead of the previous `~59-60 tok/s` band.

Validation:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 python3 -m ruff check tinygrad/llm/amd_kimi.py /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_shared_gateup_rowgroup.py
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 python3 -m unittest test.unit.test_llm_moe
```

## 2026-05-20 Router Warp64 Kept

Question: after the warp32 router topk win, can `kimi_router_topk` use the full gfx942 wave64? Unlike one-row GEMV kernels, router topk does not change an FP dot/reduction order. It computes each expert's sigmoid independently, compares `(score, index)` pairs, and only sums the final 8 probabilities in the same lane0 order. So `THREADS=64, PER_LANE=6` should preserve output while reducing local scan work and using the whole wave.

Added durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_router_warp64.py
```

```text
kimi_router_warp64 n=8200 med=18.200 us avg=18.468 us
```

Integrated production `kimi_router_topk`:

```text
THREADS = 64
PER_LANE = 6
shuffle width = 64
reduction deltas = 32,16,8,4,2,1
```

Post-patch microbench:

```text

prob_diff 0.0
kimi_router_topk   n=8200 med=17.560 us avg=17.763 us
kimi_router_warp64 n=8200 med=17.480 us avg=17.677 us
```

Full Kimi sanity:

```sh

CUSTOM_KIMI_MOE_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_SHARED_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ROUTER_TOPK=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_OUTPUT_ARGMAX_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ATTN_QAKV_HALF=1 \
```

## 2026-05-20 Down Wave64 Pair Kept

Question: current selected down uses `WARPS_PER_ROW=2`. The two 32-lane subwarps for one output row sit in the same wave64. Can lane0 read lane32's partial through `__shfl(sum, 32, 64)` and remove the `partial[TOPK * ROWS * WARPS_PER_ROW]` LDS plus final `__syncthreads()`?

Added durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_down_wave64_pair.py
```

The variant keeps:

```text
ROWS=8, WARPS_PER_ROW=2
xps/xss Q8 staging
per-subwarp 32-lane sum reduction
total = sum0 + sum1
acc += total * probs[k] for k=0..7
```

It removes only the LDS partial write/read and the final sync.

## 2026-05-20 Router Warp64 Followups Rejected

Question: after switching production `kimi_router_topk` to warp64, do the old warp32 followups change? Retested:

- `lane0_local`: lane0 keeps selected probabilities in registers/private storage and writes all outputs.
- `lane0_global`: lane0 writes unnormalized probabilities to global output, then normalizes in place.
- `noidx`: do not store the local expert index array; recompute `lane + t * 64`.
- `recompute`: store only scores and recompute sigmoid probability for the final 8 winners.


Conclusion: the obvious constants/unroll knobs in `amd_kimi.py` are already locally optimized. The remaining path to 65 tok/s is unlikely to come from these knobs; it needs structural work outside simple per-kernel constants, especially reducing non-custom tinygrad graph kernels or fusing attention/RMS/residual style kernels.

## 2026-05-20 Router+Quant Wave64 Q8 Pair Rejected

Question: `kimi_router_quant_q8_0` launches 64-thread CTAs for the fused router+Q8 path, but the quant branch uses only `tid < 32`, so half of each wave64 is idle for the `DIM/32` Q8 blocks. Tested a variant that lets each 64-thread CTA quantize two Q8 blocks using `slot = tid >> 5`, reducing quant CTAs from `224` to `112` while keeping the router CTA unchanged.

Updated durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_router_quant_threads.py
```

Command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. ITERS=4096 \
python3 /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_router_quant_threads.py
```

Correctness:

```text
diff32 0.0 0
diff64 0.0 0
diff64_qpair 0.0 0
```

Wall output:

```text
current       1706.29 us
t32           1654.47 us
t64           1727.28 us
t64_qpair     1706.79 us
```

PROFILE medians from `/tmp/b1/profile.pkl.b1tg`:

```text
bench_router_quant_t32           n=8200 med=19.400 us avg=19.625 us
bench_router_quant_t64           n=8200 med=17.560 us avg=17.736 us
bench_router_quant_t64_qpair     n=8200 med=17.480 us avg=17.687 us
kimi_router_quant_q8_0           n=8200 med=17.560 us avg=17.763 us
```

Decision: reject production change. The qpair shape is exact but only saves about `0.08 us/layer`, or roughly `0.005 ms/token` across 60 MoE layers, far below the current 65 tok/s gap. Keep the current production fused router+quant shape.

## 2026-05-20 Output Q8 Preshuffle Rejected

Question: previous output argmax `load32` used unaligned int loads from the GGUF Q8_0 row layout and tied current byte-pack. Tested a stronger load-time layout idea for `output.weight`: split each Q8 block into separate scale bytes plus aligned `int32[8]` payload, so `kimi_output_argmax_q8_0_stage1` can remove weight byte loads and `pack4_i8(...)` work.

Durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_output_argmax_preshuffle.py
```

The bench uses equivalent synthetic all-one Q8 data:

```text
raw layout:   [f16 scale][32 q bytes] per block
split layout: [f16 scale bytes] + aligned int32 payload
```

Command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. ITERS=64 \
python3 /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_output_argmax_preshuffle.py
```

Correctness:

```text
split_diff 0.0 0
xpack_diff 1.4417091733776033e-06 30
```

Wall output was misleading under this non-TinyJit synthetic loop:

```text
current       5407.76 us
split         2989.75 us
split_xpack   4335.76 us
```

PROFILE medians from `/tmp/b1/profile.pkl.b1tg`:

```text
kimi_output_argmax_q8_0_stage1  n=136 med= 305.580 us avg= 304.893 us
bench_output_stage1_split       n=136 med= 325.100 us avg= 320.152 us
bench_output_stage1_split_xpack n=136 med=1362.640 us avg=1365.448 us
```

Decision: reject. Aligned output Q8 preshuffle is exact for the non-xpack variant, but kernel time is about `20 us/token` slower than current. The xpack variant is much slower and not tie-exact on equal logits. No production code change.


## 2026-05-20 Q4 Preshuffle Add/Xor Recheck

Question: the earlier Q4 expert preshuffle bench used the old `q4sign` nibble unpack in the split/pair kernels, while production now uses the faster add/xor unpack. Rechecked the layout signal with split/pair kernels updated to:

```c
q4lo(p) = ((p & 0x0f0f0f0f) + 0x78787878) ^ 0x80808080
q4hi(p) = (((p >> 4) & 0x0f0f0f0f) + 0x78787878) ^ 0x80808080
```

Updated durable bench:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_q4_preshuffle.py
```

Command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. ITERS=64 EXPERTS=384 \
python3 /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_q4_preshuffle.py
```

Wall output still favors current because the synthetic loop pays more launch/scheduler/input overhead for the split layout:

```text
gate_current         926.06 us
gate_split          1071.57 us
gate_pair           1169.27 us
down_current        1501.40 us
down_split          1512.83 us
```

PROFILE medians from `/tmp/b1/profile.pkl.b1tg` show the kernel-level layout signal:

```text
kimi_gate_up_q4_q8_to_q8_0  n=67 med=39.560 us avg=40.491 us
bench_gateup_q4_split       n=67 med=36.360 us avg=37.072 us
bench_gateup_q4_pair        n=67 med=36.920 us avg=38.045 us
kimi_down_reduce_q4_q8_0    n=67 med=24.760 us avg=25.340 us
bench_down_q4_split         n=67 med=26.360 us avg=27.387 us
```

Interpretation:

- Gate/up split layout is now the best preshuffle shape: about `3.2 us/layer` faster than current in kernel timestamp.
- Across roughly 60 selected-MoE layers, this is about `0.19 ms/token`, which is finally the same order as the current `64.3 -> 65 tok/s` gap.
- Down split remains worse; do not split down.
- This is not a production change yet. To be useful, the GGUF load path must create split gate/up Q4 tensors without keeping duplicate raw expert weights. A duplicate-layout full-model test is not acceptable on memory, and the synthetic wall loop is not a decode proof.

Decision: keep as the next serious 65 tok/s route, not as a committed production optimization yet. Next proof should use one real Kimi layer with raw-to-split transform and exact output comparison, then design a no-duplicate loader path for gate/up only.

Validation:

```sh
ruff check /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_q4_preshuffle.py
```

## 2026-05-20 Packed Q4 Gate/Up Split Rejected Due Quality

Follow-up from the Q4 preshuffle add/xor recheck: the plain split layout had a real kernel win, but the direct four-buffer split shape made TinyJit replay slower because gate/up became four graph inputs instead of two. Tested a packed split shape that preserves two weight buffers:

```text
packed gate raw = [all f16 scales][all 16-byte qdata blocks]
packed up raw   = [all f16 scales][all 16-byte qdata blocks]
```

Durable real-layer proof:

```text
/home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_q4_preshuffle_real_layer.py
```

Command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 NO_COLOR=1 PROFILE=1 JITBEAM=2 PYTHONPATH=. LAYER=1 EXPERTS=384 ITERS=64 REPS=5 \
python3 /home/b1tg/back/tinygrad/kimi/bench_q4_preshuffle_real_layer.py
```

Isolated correctness:

```text
q8_diff 0 pack_diff 0
```

TinyJit wall:

```text
jit_current  median=224.85 us
jit_pack     median=220.75 us
```

PROFILE medians:

```text
kimi_gate_up_q4_q8_to_q8_0       n=647 med=43.920 us avg=44.266 us
bench_gateup_q4_split_pack       n=647 med=39.920 us avg=40.284 us
```

Temporarily integrated behind:

```text
CUSTOM_KIMI_GATEUP_SPLIT_Q4=1
```

Temporary implementation:

- `tinygrad/llm/gguf.py`: when the flag is enabled for Q4 `ffn_gate_exps.weight` and `ffn_up_exps.weight`, build `_ggml_raw_split` as packed split layout and use a dummy lazy f16 tensor for the unused dequantized expert weight. This avoids retaining the original raw gate/up buffer.
- `tinygrad/llm/amd_kimi.py`: add `kimi_gate_up_q4_q8_to_q8_split_from_q8_0` and `kimi_gate_up_q4_q8_to_q8_split_from_sel_probs_q8_0`.
- `tinygrad/llm/model.py`: in the Kimi Q8 selected MoE path, use the packed split gate/up kernel when `_ggml_raw_split` is present.
- `tinygrad/nn/state.py`: preserve `_ggml_raw_split` through `load_state_dict`.

Full Kimi command:

```sh
TMPDIR=/tmp/b1 TMP=/tmp/b1 TEMP=/tmp/b1 JITBEAM=9 REALIZE_SMALL=8000000 \
CUSTOM_KIMI_MOE_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_SHARED_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ROUTER_TOPK=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ROUTER_QUANT_Q8=1 \
CUSTOM_KIMI_OUTPUT_ARGMAX_Q8=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_ATTN_QAKV_HALF=1 CUSTOM_KIMI_GATEUP_SPLIT_Q4=1 \
PYTHONPATH=. python3 tinygrad/llm/cli.py \
  --model /raid/gguf/kimi/Kimi-K2.6-UD-Q4_K_XL-00001-of-00014.gguf \
  --shard 3 --benchmark 8
```

Result:

```text
using model "Kimi-K2.6" with 205,427,043,328 bytes and 1,027,331,700,224 params
[BOS]-than . . . . . . .
15.63 ms, 63.99 tok/s
15.27 ms, 65.50 tok/s
15.27 ms, 65.47 tok/s
15.29 ms, 65.39 tok/s
15.29 ms, 65.40 tok/s
```

Quality A/B showed this is not acceptable. Baseline without `CUSTOM_KIMI_GATEUP_SPLIT_Q4` on the same flags produced the old token trajectory:

```text
[BOS] | `emppen`='001
```

The split flag produced:

```text
[BOS]-than . . . . . . .
```

Disabling `CUSTOM_KIMI_ROUTER_QUANT_Q8` still produced the bad split trajectory, so the regression is not limited to the `sel_probs` split variant. Additional isolated checks passed:

```text
layer 1/2/20/40/60: q8_diff 0 pack_diff 0 sel_probs_pack_diff 0
layer 1 all experts: all_expert_pack_diff 0
```

Interpretation: the packed split kernel remains a real isolated speed signal, but the end-to-end model output changes under the load-time dummy-weight/no-duplicate integration. Quality is not acceptable, so the production integration was reverted. Keep only the durable bench evidence for future debugging; do not use this flag as a speed path.

Decision: reject. 65 tok/s is not achieved under the quality requirement.

```text
15.70 ms, 63.71 tok/s
15.59 ms, 64.14 tok/s
15.62 ms, 64.04 tok/s
15.63 ms, 63.98 tok/s
15.70 ms, 63.71 tok/s
```
